# Evidence register

Sources: [2-pager](../papers/proposal-2-pager.pdf) sections 2, 3 and 5; [Santos et al.](../papers/santos-2024.pdf) section 7 for R1.

The results register: one entry per experiment, reproduction or paid generation run, holding its configuration, its result and what the
result was read against. The criteria live in [RESEARCH.md](RESEARCH.md); an entry links the
section that holds them rather than restating them. An interactive session of
[`bko.app`](../src/bko/app/README.md) is not a run of record: it reads no criterion, and its run
folder is its record.

## How to read an entry

| Field | Meaning |
|---|---|
| Status | `not yet run`, `running` or `complete` |
| Question | What the experiment answers |
| Configuration | Everything needed to rerun it: dataset and split with the dataset snapshot's identity (download date and checksum; for Synthea its `run.json`: jar checksum, seeds, snapshot date, population size and CSV checksums), the generator's model id, sampling parameters and prompt, baselines and controls, metrics and library versions, seeds, the commit, and how the readings [RESEARCH.md](RESEARCH.md#4-success-criteria) leaves open were fixed |
| Runs | One dated row per run: run id, date, commit, where its logs and artifacts live (its run folder under `runs/`, or the `scratchpad/` paths a row names; [DEVELOPMENT.md](DEVELOPMENT.md#runs)), and, for runs that call a paid API, its API spend, the model the API reported, its token counts and where its request/response log lives. A rerun adds a row; a superseded or failed run keeps its row with the reason |
| Result | What was measured, with units, and the command that produced it. Empty until the status is `complete` |
| Read against | The RESEARCH.md section whose criteria judge the result, and the verdict: met, not met, inconclusive (its intervals decide neither way), or a null result reported as such |

## R1. MONDO and DO fusion reproduction

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Status | `complete` |
| Question | Does `bko.bkb`, given MONDO and DO fragments built from the pinned releases, rebuild the fusion example of Santos et al. (section 7 of [the paper](../papers/santos-2024.pdf)), and where does it differ? |
| Configuration | Command: `uv run --no-sync python -m bko.ontology reproduce`. Code: `bko.ontology.santos` holds the paper's terms, PTAs, anchor, passes and misprints; `bko.ontology.fragments` the encoding (ADR `research/bko-fragment-encoding`). Environment: Python 3.14, pronto 2.7.3 (`uv.lock`). Releases: MONDO 2026-10-06 and DO 2026-09-30 as `bko.datasets.sources` pins them, where the paper used MONDO 2023-09-12 and DO 2023-10-21. Bridge: MONDO's `skos:exactMatch` annotations, where the paper queried the EMBL-EBI OxO service. Fusion: weight 1 per fragment, `fuse`'s default (each source prior over every fragment's weight, the paper's section 6.1). Instantiation: from the paper's anchor (MONDO's lesion of sciatic nerve, DO's sciatic neuropathy), each pass adds every rule whose antecedents hold, as the paper's algorithm states. Misprints read as the releases give them: Table 1's DOID:114466 is DOID:11446; the fifth MONDO PTA is printed as a second TM4 with antecedent Mononeuritis, and MONDO gives neuritis (MONDO:0002122) as the subclass of inflammatory disease; section 7.1 narrates two bridge rules in its second pass whose antecedents are the anchor |
| Runs | `r1-1` · 2026-10-17 · commit `a8ddddb` on `feat/mondo-do-fusion` (kept in the PR's history) · superseded by `r1-2`: review fixes changed `bko.ontology.santos` after it · no API spend<br>`r1-2` · 2026-10-17 · commit `05e93b3` on `feat/mondo-do-fusion` (kept in the PR's history) · stdout and stderr in `scratchpad/reproduce.csv` and `scratchpad/reproduce.err` on the runner's machine (gitignored); the command rewrites them byte for byte, and its stdout is `r1-1`'s · no API spend<br>`r1-3` · 2026-10-19 · commit `e4a5507` on `feat/second-machine-reproduction` · `uv run --no-sync python -m bko.ontology reproduce > scratchpad/pr1-reproduce.csv 2> scratchpad/pr1-reproduce.err </dev/null`, the command `make reproduce` runs; exit 0 · a second machine, Linux x86_64 under WSL2, with Python 3.14.4 and pronto 2.7.3 (`uv.lock`); releases fetched on that machine: MONDO `releases/2026-10-06`, sha256 `2631e8e165d41def1a46749adb1ce0a336c8aba7f6bc0978729592cee8fed60b`, and DO `releases/2026-09-30`, sha256 `51e717b6b9f5d391f2793f9e16faf9c8629a661ab8f21c3a024458129f182ad2` · stdout and stderr in `scratchpad/pr1-reproduce.csv` and `scratchpad/pr1-reproduce.err` on the runner's machine (gitignored), sha256 `7a059dce04ee11cb64df42254171a56c0d807adf1273c4eded27c690f187631e` and `1761217fd505e97a58c72f3bcd63bb9922b3e977b1451df45ee78b64a08a0e04` · 40 rows: 34 `match`, 4 `erratum`, 2 `reported`, none `differs`; R1 records no output checksum, so it is compared with `r1-2` by row status: no row `differs` of 40, as the Result states, and the `erratum` and `reported` rows are the ones the Result names, with the same measured values · no API spend |
| Result | `make reproduce`: no row `differs` of 40. The 11 terms and 17 PTAs are found; DOID:114466 and the fifth MONDO PTA are `erratum` rows. Instantiation from the anchor adds TM1, TD1, TB2 and TB3, then TM2, TD2, TB1 and TB4, where section 7.1 narrates TM1 and TD1, then TM2, TD2 and TB1 to TB4 (`erratum`: TB2 and TB3 take the anchor as their antecedents); the same 8 after two passes, and all 17 at the fixpoint. Inferences a and b: 0.3333333333333333 each against the paper's 0.33; under pybkb's normalization a is 0.5, so the paper's figure follows from its algorithm's every-fragment denominator. The chain DOID:11446 -> DOID:12528 -> DOID:9473 -> DOID:1802 -> MONDO:0002121 -> MONDO:0002122 -> MONDO:0021166 exists in the fused BKB and in neither fragment nor the whole MONDO release; its product is 0.001371742112482853, one source prior of 1/3 per S-node, where the paper gives no figure. Beyond the command: the fused BKB is not mutually exclusive, since MONDO's two S-nodes on mononeuropathy share their source (`tests/test_ontology_santos.py`); and the chain's first two DO steps are axioms the pinned MONDO excludes: `relationship: excluded_subClassOf MONDO:0001543 {source="DOID:11446", source="https://orcid.org/0000-0001-5208-3432"} ! lesion of sciatic nerve` and `relationship: excluded_subClassOf MONDO:0004797 {source="DOID:12528", source="https://orcid.org/0000-0001-5208-3432"} ! mononeuritis of lower limb` |
| Read against | [RESEARCH.md: Detect](RESEARCH.md#detect); verdict: met |

## E1. UCI Heart Disease augmentation

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Status | `complete` |
| Question | Does BKO-guided augmentation on UCI Heart Disease meet the success criteria against the baselines and controls? |
| Configuration | `bko.evaluate.experiment.E1` holds the values and ADR [`research/e1-evaluation-protocol`](adr/research/e1-evaluation-protocol.md) the protocol and its reasons. Data: the UCI archive `bko.datasets.sources` names, split by `bko.datasets.split` at each of `E1`'s holdout seeds. Arms: no augmentation, class weighting, SMOTE, ADASYN, CTGAN, TVAE and three BKO arms (`bko`, the candidate, and `bko-population` and `bko-frequency`, which no criterion reads and whose measures are reported beside it); every arm is asked to fill every underrepresented cell up to the threshold of [RESEARCH.md](RESEARCH.md#2-intended-users-and-evaluation-setting) from the holdout's training rows; every row-adding arm, the four baselines and the three BKO arms, also fills a replica of the training split's cells ([RESEARCH.md section 5](RESEARCH.md#5-evaluation-contract)); a refused fill is retried with a derived seed, up to `E1`'s attempts. BKO arms (`bko.evaluate.guided`): each reads only the holdout's training rows, finds their gaps under its definition (`bko` per-subgroup, `bko-population` population-level, `bko-frequency` frequency-aware; ADR [`research/bko-formal-gap-definition`](adr/research/bko-formal-gap-definition.md)), guides each requested cell by them, asks the Messages API for each cell's shortfall round by round, validates every reply with `bko.validate`, drops each accepted candidate `bko.baselines.records.check` would refuse (by `bko.baselines.records.drops`: outside the training split's range, then in a cell the request does not ask for, then past its cell's quota), counts a candidate toward the cell its own values name, and returns the rest without clipping, rounding or repairing a value. Each BKO arm fills the replica request the same way. On the default split population-level grounding lists no gap ([E2](#e2-synthea-gap-detection-ablation)), so `bko-population` guides each cell by what its records show. Fusion: an arm's rows join the holdout's training rows (`bko.baselines.records.training_set`) before the classifier fits; gap closure after fusion is read by [C1](#c1-gap-closure-after-fusion), not here. Budget per attempt: `E1.budget` times the request's rows, counted in records asked; an attempt whose remaining budget cannot cover the rows still short is refused as a plain `BaselineError`. Spend ceiling: a run stops, unretried, once its call log's spend reaches `E1.spend_ceiling` USD; a call that gets no response also stops it. Spend and duration estimates: the `e1-4` entry of Runs holds the estimate stated before the judged run. Generator: `bko.generate.messages.SETTINGS`, `Settings("claude-haiku-5-5", "medium", 16000, 20, Level.SUMMARY)`, for all three arms, with the training-split bounds in the prompt: the level [G3](#g3-guidance-level) chose and the effort [G4](#g4-effort-at-summary) chose, as [G1](#g1-record-generation-through-the-messages-api) records them; no sampling parameter is sent and the API takes no seed; the prompt is `bko.generate.prompt` at the run's commit, and each call's request is in the call log. Classifier: `bko.evaluate.classifier`; the criteria read its logistic regression, and a histogram gradient boosting model fit on the same augmented rows on one OpenMP thread (threadpoolctl as `uv.lock` pins it) is scored beside it as `recall-hgb`, `precision-hgb` and `auc-pr-hgb` rows, its primary and secondary contrasts logged by `compare` as `contrast model=hgb` lines with no verdict; replica utility reads the regression only; ADR [`research/e1-evaluation-protocol`](adr/research/e1-evaluation-protocol.md) holds its settings and why no criterion reads it. Test split: on each holdout every arm fills before any is scored; a test record equal in every column to a row some arm added or replicated leaves the test split every arm's utility is scored on, and each arm's count of such rows is reported (`test-copies`); the held-out ratio and the test rows' distance to closest record use the whole split. How the criteria of [RESEARCH.md](RESEARCH.md#4-success-criteria) are read: primary, the candidate beats every comparator, however slightly, in both recall and AUC-PR over the whole test split, each paired difference's corrected interval lying above zero (`E1`'s margin is zero); secondary, the same over the flagged subgroups, the age-sex subgroups that hold an underrepresented cell; privacy, the 5th percentile of the added rows' distance to the training rows reaches `E1`'s share of the test rows' 5th percentile, its interval lying above it; completeness comes from the fusion stage ([C1](#c1-gap-closure-after-fusion)), not from this harness; validity is `bko`'s `passed` summed over `generated` summed across every `bko.validate` call it makes, in its gap and replica fills alike, on every holdout and in every refused attempt, a candidate dropped afterwards included, met when that rate is at least `E1.validity` and not met below it, read as a point with no interval, and refused when a fill generated nothing; each fill's counts and rate are reported apart, with no verdict; `bko-population`'s and `bko-frequency`'s rates and every BKO arm's dropped candidates are reported beside it. An interval criterion is met when every interval it reads lies above its threshold, not met when one lies below it at the family-wise level (each of its k intervals read at confidence 1 - (1 - c)/k, c being `E1`'s confidence), and inconclusive otherwise; a `compare` that refuses the judged run's scores, as it does an empty value a criterion reads, is E1's result. Reproduction: the baselines reproduce from seeds on one machine; a BKO arm's rows come from the paid run's call log, and `score --replay` answers every call from it and rewrites the scores byte for byte with no API call (ADR [`research/call-log-replay`](adr/research/call-log-replay.md)). Judged result: E1's latest configuration, as ADR [`research/e1-evaluation-protocol`](adr/research/e1-evaluation-protocol.md) (The latest configuration is judged) states; every run keeps its Runs row here, a failed one with its reason; `e1-4` is the judged run; `e1-3` ran `E1` at level `ontology` without the training-split bounds in the prompt, and this file at commit `48098d0` holds its Result and Read against; `e1-1` ran `E1` as it stood at its commit `a12d11c` (the BKO arms filled no replica, and `SETTINGS.model` was `claude-opus-5-5`), and this file at commit `e4a5507` holds its Result and Read against |

| Read against | [RESEARCH.md: success criteria](RESEARCH.md#4-success-criteria); verdicts: primary inconclusive, secondary inconclusive, privacy met, validity met, completeness not read here ([C1](#c1-gap-closure-after-fusion)). Inconclusive reads, under E1's Configuration, as no interval lying wholly above zero at confidence 0.95 and none lying below zero at the family-wise level: all 12 primary intervals and all 12 secondary intervals contain zero, so the run neither shows nor rules out a benefit, and `bko` is not shown better or worse than any comparator. With primary inconclusive, no benefit of knowledge guidance is shown on this frame, and this is reported as such, as RESEARCH section 4 commits. The printed `[low, high]` intervals are each read at confidence 0.95; only `refuting_high`, the upper bound at the family-wise level, decides not met, and its minimum over all 24 contrasts is +0.0140 (primary AUC-PR against none). The intervals are wide: the printed upper bounds of the primary recall intervals run from +0.0324 to +0.0508 and of the flagged-subgroup recall intervals from +0.0233 to +0.0628; at the refuting level they run from +0.0497 to +0.0716 and from +0.0335 to +0.0942, so recall benefits of those sizes are not excluded. The primary AUC-PR upper bounds, +0.0091 to +0.0245 printed and +0.0140 to +0.0337 refuting, rule out a primary AUC-PR gain larger than about 0.03. Privacy met reads as its interval lying above zero: `bko`'s added rows sit farther from the training rows, at the 5th percentile, than `E1.privacy_margin` times the test rows do. Validity met reads as 25825/25825 at or above `E1.validity` (19/20). Replica utility and fidelity carry no verdict: no criterion of [RESEARCH.md section 4](RESEARCH.md#4-success-criteria) reads them, and [RESEARCH.md section 5](RESEARCH.md#5-evaluation-contract) reports fidelity with no pass threshold. `e1-4` is judged at level `summary` with the training-split bounds in the prompt, at effort `medium`; `e1-3`, at level `ontology` without the bounds and also at effort `medium` (the `settings` of each call log's `run` line), read the same four verdicts (this file at commit `48098d0`). The level and the bounds both differ between the runs, so a difference between them is not attributable to either alone. No paired comparison of `e1-4` against `e1-3` was made; one is feasible, the runs sharing their holdouts. Beside that, as point values with no interval, `bko` at `e1-3` then `e1-4`: whole-split recall 0.8085 then 0.8107, whole-split AUC-PR 0.9107 in both, flagged-subgroup AUC-PR 0.8466 then 0.8476, replica recall 0.5874 then 0.6884, replica quality 0.7766 then 0.8194, and range drops 5840 of 31643 then 0 of 25825 (from each run's `mean`, `replica` and `validity` lines of its `criteria.log`). Replica quality 0.8194 is above `tvae`'s 0.8039, the same in both runs, and below `smote`'s 0.8869, `adasyn`'s 0.9218 and `ctgan`'s 0.8209. The primary recall intervals are wider at `e1-4` against every comparator (against none 0.0868 wide, `e1-3`'s 0.0726), and the comparators' means agree between the runs to within 0.0001. `e1-4`'s generator is `e1-3`'s Haiku 5.5, and `e1-1`'s was Opus 5.5 with no BKO replica, so a difference from `e1-1` is not attributable to either change alone. Second model: reported, no verdict (ADR `research/e1-evaluation-protocol`) [RESEARCH section 6](RESEARCH.md#limits-of-uci-heart-disease-as-the-evaluation-frame) says why this frame cannot read a smaller effect and which frame the project tests next. |

## E2. Synthea gap-detection ablation

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Status | `complete` |
| Question | Does BKO gap detection find the cases deliberately ablated from a Synthea population, and how do BKO-detected gaps compare with count-based ones? |
| Configuration | Commands: `uv run --no-sync python -m bko.gaps synthea`, and `uv run --no-sync python -m bko.gaps uci` for the UCI reading. Code: `bko.gaps.experiment` holds the values (`E2_RUN`, `E2_ABLATIONS`) and the reading; `bko.gaps.definitions` the three gap definitions of ADR [`research/bko-formal-gap-definition`](adr/research/bko-formal-gap-definition.md) and the frequency-aware rule of ADR [`research/frequency-aware-gap-score`](adr/research/frequency-aware-gap-score.md) with its constants; ADR [`research/dataset-fragment-encoding`](adr/research/dataset-fragment-encoding.md) the dataset fragments; ADR [`research/synthea-ablation-design`](adr/research/synthea-ablation-design.md) the ablation. Data: the population `E2_RUN` names (`bko.datasets.synthea_run.DEFAULT_RUN`), the patients alive on its snapshot date. Releases: MONDO as `bko.datasets.sources` pins it; no Synthea code maps to an HPO concept, so the Synthea BKO holds no HPO fragment. Scope: the classes the code mappings name and their MONDO `is_a` ancestors. Code mappings: `bko.ontology.mapping.CODE_MAPPINGS` maps seven Synthea disorder codes, ischemic heart disease, essential hypertension, type 2 diabetes, hyperlipidemia, myocardial infarction, ST-elevation myocardial infarction and stroke, each to the one live MONDO concept carrying its `skos:exactMatch` (ADR [`research/ontology-mapping-design`](adr/research/ontology-mapping-design.md)), a scope of 32 classes and 37 statements (the `scope` line of `e2-9`'s stderr); ST-elevation myocardial infarction maps by that rule beside myocardial infarction; acute non-ST-elevation myocardial infarction (SNOMED CT 401314000) is excluded (`UNMAPPED_CODES`), since no live MONDO concept carries its `skos:exactMatch`; `e2-0` to `e2-8` ran with ischemic heart disease alone (MONDO:0024644, 9 statements). Fusion: weight 1 per fragment, `fuse`'s default normalization; the gap lists do not depend on the weights. Ablations: `cell`, `DEFAULT_ABLATION`'s band and sex, and `condition`, every band and both sexes, each removing every patient with `ISCHEMIC_HEART_DISEASE` it selects, and the same two at `REMOVED_FRACTIONS`, named `cell-25`, `cell-50`, `cell-75`, `condition-25`, `condition-50` and `condition-75`, which remove that share of the selected patients, the first of a draw seeded by the spec's seed (`bko.datasets.ablation`), whole patients each. Sensitivity and specificity: per detector, sensitivity is the share of the eligible truth gaps recovered, and specificity the share of the items outside the truth, every (cell, statement) pair of the grid for a definition and every age-sex-label cell for the count-based detector, that are not new outside it; both read `n/a` when their denominator is empty (`bko.gaps.experiment.detection`, the `detection` lines of stderr). Cells: every detector reads the ablation's age bands, `SYNTHEA_AGE_BAND_EDGES`; the definitions read the 12 age-sex cells, which carry no label because the label is the ablated condition, and the count-based detector the 24 age-sex-label cells with the threshold of [RESEARCH.md](RESEARCH.md#2-intended-users-and-evaluation-setting). Definitions: per-subgroup grounds each cell against its own patients; population-level grounds every patient together, its gaps holding in all 12 cells; frequency-aware flags a cell's concept against every patient outside the cell. Reading, over (cell, statement) pairs for each definition and over cells for the count-based detector: the truth is the pairs whose statement the removed patients of the cell ground, whether or not the cell's other patients ground it too, and the cells whose count fell; a truth gap present before the removal is not eligible; an eligible gap present after it is recovered; a gap after the removal that lies outside the truth is outside it, and new outside it when it was absent before; a gap present before and absent after vanished. The reading and both ablations were fixed after the planning measurement `e2-0`; the per-cell reading, the three definitions and the frequency-aware rule were fixed before the planning measurement `e2-3`, which computed them as their records state them; the Result reads the seven-code scope from `e2-9`, and its one-code figures from `e2-4` (whole removals), `e2-7` (fraction ablations) and `e2-8` (first flags, from `uv run --no-sync python -m bko.gaps first-flag`, which removes each whole ablation's cases one at a time in its seed's draw order; `first_flag` counts the ablation's removed cases, so for `condition` it runs over all 261). Limits: in the one-code figures (`e2-4`, `e2-7`, `e2-8`) each definition's gaps in a cell are all 9 statements or none and every pair count is a multiple of 9; seven codes map in `e2-9`, and the `cell` ablation is still one cell's decision; the `condition` ablation removes ischemic heart disease patients whole, so the other mapped conditions they hold leave with them, and its truth holds statements the remaining patients still ground, such as the `is_a` ancestors myocardial ischemia shares with myocardial infarction (myocardial disorder, heart disorder), which no grounding definition can recover: per-subgroup's 61 of 261 and the `cell` ablation's 3 of 37 in `e2-9` read that shortfall as the richer scope's consequence, not a defect; ST-elevation myocardial infarction brings acute myocardial infarction and acute disease into scope, with 4 statements no other mapped concept reaches (ST-elevation myocardial infarction to acute myocardial infarction, acute myocardial infarction to myocardial infarction and to acute disease, acute disease to human disease), and myocardial infarction reaches no statement it does not (the `is_a` closure of each mapped concept in the pinned MONDO release, read with `bko.ontology.obo.parse_obo`); removal is complete and of whole patients, so for both grounding definitions every truth pair is eligible, and every statement reads from one class, so a per-subgroup gap new after a removal lies in the truth: per-subgroup's new outside and vanished counts, its recovery of every eligible pair with one code, and the count-based detector's new outside count of 0 follow from the definitions and check the implementation; outside counts every gap after the removal outside the truth, including absences the removal did not cause, such as the cells that held no patient with the condition, so it is context, not an error rate; with one mapped concept (`e2-4`, `e2-7`) a removal of its holders only lowers every other cell's reference share, so frequency-aware's new outside count of 0 there follows from the definitions; with seven (`e2-9`) the removed patients also hold other mapped concepts, so its new outside count of 0 there is measured, as are its vanished gaps and its recovery of the `cell` truth throughout; the partial ablations measure sensitivity and specificity; below a fraction of 1 a grounding definition sees a cell only where the draw removes every case of it, since one remaining record of the cell, or of the population, grounds a statement; the fractions of one spec share its seed-0 draw, so each removes the cases of every smaller fraction; E2 draws no cell whose population shows a concept the cell's records miss, a chance that for records drawn independently is (1 - p)^n at share p in n records (ADR `research/bko-formal-gap-definition`, Consequences); it reads one population seed with no interval; the frequency-aware reference carries no label on Synthea, so its gaps before the removals, all in age bands under 50, partly reflect incidence rising with age. Environment: Python 3.14, pronto 2.7.3, pandas 2.3.3 and scipy 1.18.1 (uv.lock). Population: run.json jar sha256 ed43c20ad40ba5c3bc724503a5af032715fe3c491620b766148e7c2361e6ecc1; seed 1, clinician seed 1, population 2000, snapshot 2026-01-01, Massachusetts; patients.csv sha256 03cbd10f710b340f05467bc674f5e6c694a9e467f67147c314f07e73d2ed5492, conditions.csv sha256 29268055a12ee7892d9e5e13366e139709fcabb7b4fba04623d2d03528faf804. MONDO releases/2026-10-06. |

| Result | `python -m bko.gaps synthea`, from its `recovery` lines, as recovered of eligible (outside the truth, new outside it, vanished). One mapped code (`e2-4`): `cell` ablation (51 patients removed; truth 9 pairs, all in men aged 60 to 69): per-subgroup 9 of 9 (27, 0, 0), its 27 outside pairs being the 3 cells that held no patient with the condition; population-level 0 of 9 (0, 0, 0), since 210 remaining patients still ground the statements; frequency-aware 9 of 9 (36, 0, 0), the cell's 61 remaining men showing the concept in no record against 210 of the 1888 patients outside it (its `flagged` line, posterior mass 0.9993); count-based 1 of 1 cell (8, 0, 0). `condition` ablation (261 removed; truth 81 pairs over the 9 cells holding a removed patient): per-subgroup 81 of 81 (27, 0, 0); population-level 81 of 81 (27, 27, 0), its new outside pairs being the 3 cells that held no patient with the condition, where its gaps now hold too; frequency-aware 0 of 72 (0, 0, 36), every reference share falling to 0 so that nothing is flagged after it, the 9 truth pairs of men aged 40 to 49 being its gaps before; count-based 7 of 7 eligible cells (6, 0, 0), 2 of its 9 truth cells holding fewer records than the threshold before the removal. Before either removal (`e2-4`) per-subgroup lists 27 pairs, population-level none, and frequency-aware 36 (its 4 flagged cells, all in age bands under 50). Fraction ablations (`e2-7`; every detector's specificity 1 at every fraction, and no new outside or vanished gap): `cell-25`, `cell-50` and `cell-75` (13, 26 and 39 of the cell's 51 cases removed) leave per-subgroup, population-level and frequency-aware at 0 of 9 each (outside 27, 0 and 36), the cell's remaining cases still grounding and showing the concept, and the count-based detector at 0, 0 and 1 of 1 cell (outside 8); `condition-25`, `condition-50` and `condition-75` (66, 131 and 196 of 261 removed) leave per-subgroup at 0, 0 and 9 of 81, the 9 at `condition-75` being men aged 40 to 49, whose 5 cases the draw removed every one of, population-level at 0 of 81 each, frequency-aware at 0 of 72 each (outside 27), and the count-based detector at 2, 4 and 7 of 7 eligible cells (outside 6), sensitivity 0.2857, 0.5714 and 1. First flags (`e2-8`, its `first-flag` lines, as removed cases at the first listing): in `cell`, frequency-aware lists the 9 pairs at 49 of 51, when 2 cases remain, per-subgroup at 51, the cell's last case, population-level never, and the count-based detector its cell at 32; in `condition`, per-subgroup lists each cell's 9 pairs when the draw removes that cell's last case, between 181 (men aged 40 to 49, 5 cases) and 261, population-level every cell at 261, frequency-aware nothing after the removal begins, its 9 pairs of men aged 40 to 49 being listed at 0 and unlisted once (1 reversal each), and the count-based detector its cells between 0 (the 2 cells under the threshold before) and 172. These agree with every fraction ablation's recovery. Seven mapped codes (`e2-9`; `gaps rows=4921`): before either removal per-subgroup lists 93 pairs, population-level none, and frequency-aware 99, from 21 flagged (cell, concept) pairs in the 4 cells under 50. `cell` ablation (51 removed; truth 37 pairs, all in men aged 60 to 69): per-subgroup 3 of 37 (93, 0, 0), the 3 statements of the scope only myocardial ischemia reaches (myocardial ischemia to ischemic disease and to myocardial disorder, ischemic disease to vascular disorder), sensitivity 0.0811; population-level 0 of 37 (0, 0, 0); frequency-aware 3 of 37 (88, 0, 11); count-based 1 of 1 cell (8, 0, 0); every specificity 1. `condition` ablation (261 removed; truth 261 pairs over the 9 cells holding a removed patient): per-subgroup 61 of 261 (93, 0, 0), sensitivity 0.2337, which the recovered rows of `runs/e2-9/gaps-synthea.csv` (`after` and `truth`, not `before`) split by cell: the 3 myocardial-ischemia-only statements in each of the 9 cells, plus myocardial disorder to heart disorder and heart disorder to cardiovascular disorder in men aged 40 to 49, the 8 stroke-only statements in women aged 70 to 79, the 4 ST-elevation-only statements in men aged 70 to 79 and in women aged 80 and over, and in men aged 80 and over the stroke, ST-elevation and 3 hypertension-only statements and vascular disorder to cardiovascular disorder; population-level 27 of 261 (9, 9, 0), the 3 statements only myocardial ischemia reaches, in each of the 9 cells, its new outside pairs being the same 3 in the 3 cells the ablation removes no case from (women and men under 40, women aged 40 to 49; the `first-flag` log lists the other 9 cells, whose cases sum to 261), sensitivity 0.1034, specificity 0.9508; frequency-aware 0 of 256 (68, 0, 31), its 5 ineligible truth pairs being gaps of men aged 40 to 49 before the removal, and its 31 vanished pairs all in cells under 50; count-based 7 of 7 eligible cells (6, 0, 0). Fractions (`e2-9`; every specificity 1, no new outside gap): `cell-25`, `cell-50` and `cell-75` leave every definition at 0 of 25, 29 and 29, frequency-aware vanishing 11 pairs at `cell-50` and `cell-75`, and the count-based detector at 0, 0 and 1 of 1 cell; `condition-25`, `condition-50` and `condition-75` leave per-subgroup at 4 of 217, 20 of 245 and 29 of 257 (sensitivity 0.0184, 0.0816 and 0.1128), population-level at 0 each, frequency-aware at 0 of 212, 240 and 252 eligible with 11, 13 and 13 pairs vanished, and the count-based detector at 2, 4 and 7 of 7 eligible cells. First flags (`e2-9`, `gaps rows=904`): in `cell`, frequency-aware lists its 3 pairs at 49 of 51, per-subgroup at 51, population-level never, and the count-based detector its cell at 32, as with one code; in `condition`, per-subgroup lists 61 of the truth items, the earliest at 52 (women aged 80 and over) and in men aged 40 to 49 at 181, population-level 3 items per cell at 261, frequency-aware only the 5 items of men aged 40 to 49 it lists before any removal, and the count-based detector its cells at the same removed counts as with one code; these agree with every `e2-9` fraction ablation's recovery. BKO instantiation ran on the fused MONDO, HPO and dataset fragments (the `uci` run's population-level `fused fragments=3` line, named `uci-heart-disease`), so ADR `research/bko-formal-gap-definition`'s reversal condition did not occur; frequency-aware recovers 9 of 9 eligible `cell` pairs in `e2-4` and 3 of 37 in `e2-9`, so ADR `research/frequency-aware-gap-score`'s second reversal condition does not take it out of E1, and its rule is fixed from `e2-4`. On UCI Heart Disease, `python -m bko.gaps uci` lists no population-level gap in the training split, since every concept the field mappings name is shown by at least one training record; 16 per-subgroup pairs in 2 cells, 14 of them in the `<55`, female, target 1 cell, which holds 2 records and is one of the 2 count-based cells, and 2 in the `<55`, female, target 0 cell; 13 of the 16 are also frequency-aware gaps, the other 3 being the chain of a concept the 2-record cell does not show and the frequency-aware rule does not flag at that size; 21 frequency-aware pairs in 5 cells from 5 flagged (cell, concept) pairs, two of them with posterior mass within 0.003 of the 0.95 level (0.9527 and 0.9523); and 2 count-based cells. |

The 2-pager sets no pass threshold for this experiment; verdict: reported, no criterion applies |

## E3. Synthea downstream augmentation

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Status | `complete` |
| Question | On a Synthea frame whose training rows lost most of one cell's cases, how much recall on that cell's held-out cases does each augmentation method recover, beside the un-ablated training split and the ablated one unaugmented? |
| Configuration | ADR [`research/synthea-downstream-design`](adr/research/synthea-downstream-design.md) holds the frame, the removal, the reference arm, the copy rule and their reasons; `bko.evaluate.experiment.E3` holds the values. Data: the Synthea population `ed43c20ad40ba5c3-s1-cs1-p2000-d20260101-massachusetts` under `data/synthea/` (the `population` line of `scores.log`), whose `run.json` reads jar sha256 `ed43c20ad40ba5c3bc724503a5af032715fe3c491620b766148e7c2361e6ecc1`, seed 1, clinician seed 1, population 2000, snapshot 2026-01-01, Massachusetts, patients.csv sha256 `03cbd10f710b340f05467bc674f5e6c694a9e467f67147c314f07e73d2ed5492` (2283 rows) and conditions.csv sha256 `29268055a12ee7892d9e5e13366e139709fcabb7b4fba04623d2d03528faf804` (211860 rows), the population [E2](#e2-synthea-gap-detection-ablation) reads; `run.json` sha256 `35a8e8fdf5a9b357c940b7a04e6087f9553b1638acfbb30be8e9885acb06129b`. Frame: the patients alive on the snapshot date and aged 40 and over, 985 records, digest `f9c6a23e30036a12a8647903783cac6cd47eac3f58e40d82b09b7d572898b94f`, from the `data dataset=synthea frame=synthea records=985 digest=` line of the rehearsal `e3-1`'s `scores.log`; the ADR's [C12] command prints the same digest at this entry's commit. Release: MONDO as `bko.datasets.sources` pins it; each slice's `scores.log` reads its `snapshot present mondo` line with `sha256=2631e8e165d41def1a46749adb1ce0a336c8aba7f6bc0978729592cee8fed60b` and `release mondo data_version=releases/2026-10-06 snapshot=2631e8e165d41def terms=63329 obsolete=4639`. Split: E1's cell-stratified split at holdout seeds 0 to 99. Removal (`E3.removal`): 0.9 of cell `(>=65, female, 1)`'s training rows leave each holdout's training split, 47 of 52, and the test split is never thinned. Arms (`E3.arms`): `none`, `class-weight`, `smote`, `adasyn`, `ctgan`, `tvae`, `bko`, `bko-population`, `bko-frequency` and `real`, which adds the removed rows back, so its training set is the un-ablated split. Replica arms (`E3.replica_arms`): the row-adding methods (`ADDS_ROWS`) and the three BKO arms. Generator: `bko.generate.messages.SETTINGS`, `Settings(model="claude-haiku-5-5", effort="medium", max_tokens=16000, per_call=20, guidance=Level.SUMMARY)`, effort `medium` being [G4](#g4-effort-at-summary)'s choice, for all three BKO arms. Prompt: `bko.generate.prompt` at level `summary` over the Synthea frame; the ADR's Egress says what each call sends. Library versions: `uv.lock` at the run commit, which each slice's `versions` line records. Criteria: none (`E3.criteria` is empty). Copies: counted, not removed (`E3.decontaminate` is false): a test record an arm copies stays in the test split every arm is scored on, each holdout writes a `copies-kept` held-out row, and `compare` pairs with `allow_copies` (the ADR, Decision). Slices: the named runs `e3-a` (holdouts 0 to 49) and `e3-b` (holdouts 50 to 99), each with a ceiling of $32.50 (`E3_SLICE_CEILING`), 2 x $32.50 = $65.00 (`E3.spend_ceiling`), launched concurrently under the paid-run protocol ([DEVELOPMENT.md](DEVELOPMENT.md#evaluation)) with `--in-flight 8` each, as run ids `e3-2` (`e3-a`) and `e3-3` (`e3-b`); `merge e3` joins their `scores.csv` files in seed order into the bytes one `e3` run writes and writes them to stdout, which `runs/e3-4/scores.csv` holds by redirect, and `compare e3` reads the merged file. Go/no-go: the ADR re-chooses the removal before the paid run if the rehearsal's paired `none` minus `real` recall at scope `flagged-subgroups` has an interval that includes 0 (Reversal Conditions); the rehearsal `e3-1` reads -0.3515 [-0.4819, -0.2212] at confidence 0.95 over its 100 holdouts, so the removal stands. The source is the ADR's [C13] run over `runs/e3-1/rehearsal/scores.csv` at `44aaa9e`'s code, its `none vs real augmented recall flagged-subgroups` line; at this entry's commit the same figure, negated, is the `unjudged criterion=secondary scope=flagged-subgroups arm=real against=none measure=recall` line of `compare e3 runs/e3-1/rehearsal/scores.csv --candidate real`, mean 0.351538, low 0.221202, high 0.481875 (the ADR, Rationale, The rehearsal), and that command reruns it at this entry's commit; [C13] itself pairs none of `e3-1`'s holdouts at this entry's commit, since `pairable` with `allow_copies` needs the `copies-kept` rows `e3-1` lacks. Rehearsal: `e3-1` ran at commit `44aaa9e7e9877163433c698a77adefff1762b366` on `feat/synthea-frame` (kept in PR #56's history); its folder of record is `runs/e3-1/rehearsal/` (Runs). Its `scores.csv` holds no `copies-kept` row, its `scores.log` no `removal` or `flagged` line, and its `experiment` line no `seeds`, `removal` or `decontaminate` field; the run commit's code writes all of them. Each paid slice rehearses at its run commit before it launches (`launch <run-id> <run> --rehearse`).<br>Readings: `compare e3`'s comparators are E1's, `METHOD_ARMS`, so its own contrasts never read `real`. The headline comes from three commands at the run commit, and Result quotes their lines: (i) `bko` paired against `real`, `test ! -e runs/e3-4/criteria.csv && test ! -e runs/e3-4/criteria.log && uv run --no-sync python -m bko.evaluate compare e3 runs/e3-4/scores.csv --candidate bko --beside runs/e3-4/scores.csv --beside-arm real > runs/e3-4/criteria.csv 2> runs/e3-4/criteria.log`, whose `unjudged` lines also read `bko` against each comparison method, `none` included; (ii) every arm paired against `real` and against `none` over every series `pair` pairs, the ADR's [C13] over `runs/e3-4/scores.csv`, its stdout redirected to `scratchpad/e3-paired.txt` (gitignored working output; Runs records its sha256); (iii) each arm's mean headline recall, `none`, `class-weight` and `real` included, the ADR's [C14] over `runs/e3-4/scores.csv`. Scope: recall at `flagged-subgroups` counts only cases, and `(>=65, female)` is the only flagged subgroup, so it is the recall on the removed cell's test cases; AUC-PR at that scope covers the subgroup's test records of both labels |

Served model `claude-haiku-5-5` on every reply (each reply's `model` and its response's `model`), `stop_reason` `end_turn` on all 12903. The 3 undecoded records come from three short calls (`WARNING bko.generate.calls: short` lines: `e3-2`'s calls 3227 and 5288, `e3-3`'s call 5833, 1 each). `decoded` counts every item a reply returns (`bko.generate.calls`, `bko.generate.decode`), so it runs above `asked` where replies return surplus items: 361 of `e3-2`'s calls returned 444 items beyond their ask and 374 of `e3-3`'s returned 470 (113843 decoded = 113401 asked - 2 undecoded + 444; 113867 = 113398 - 1 + 470); the canned rehearsals return none, and `e1-4`'s call log reads the same rule (77489 decoded against 77487 asked). `asked` runs 1 above the 113400 quota in `e3-2` and 2 below in `e3-3`: each short call's one-record top-up adds one (the `guided` lines asking 16 and 742), and an attempt whose earlier replies' surplus records counted toward a cell before that cell's call asks one fewer (the `guided` lines asking 740: 1 in `e3-2`, 3 in `e3-3`). The harness retried tvae's fill, a local synthesizer making no API call: `tvae` and `tvae-replica` on holdouts 14 and 26 of `e3-2` and 76 of `e3-3`, `tvae-replica` alone on holdouts 4, 60 and 81; each slice retried `tvae` 2 times and `tvae-replica` 6 times, each recovered (the `WARNING bko.evaluate.run: retry` lines). Each slice's 50 holdouts log `removal holdout=<n> cell=>=65/female/1 fraction=0.9 train=788 removed=47 kept=741` and `flagged holdout=<n> cells=>=65/female/1`, and each `test holdout=` line reads `records=197` and `kept=true`, with `copied` 152 to 177 over `e3-2`'s lines and 156 to 177 over `e3-3`'s, against 146 to 167 over the rehearsal `e3-1`'s and 146 to 167 and 147 to 165 over the slices' own rehearsals. Tokens per record asked: 139.800 input and 152.358 output (`e3-2`), 137.573 and 152.942 (`e3-3`); seconds per call (request `at` to reply `at`) 9.553 mean and 9.838 median (`e3-2`), 9.581 and 9.884 (`e3-3`); the sum of call latencies over the call log's span gives a mean of 5.61 and 5.57 calls in flight.<br>Spend against the estimate: E3 spent $20.46 (10.224128000000023 + 10.231723799999957 = 20.45585179999998, the sum of each slice's `run status=` line's `usd=`, both slices one segment), 66.5% of the $30.76 estimate and under the 2 x $32.50 ceilings; each slice $10.22 and $10.23 against $15.38. The estimate overstated on both sides: input ran 139.800 and 137.573 tokens per record asked against the basis's 300.535, output 152.358 and 152.942 against 211.162; output is $8.6388 of `e3-2`'s $10.2241 (84.5%) and $8.6717 of `e3-3`'s $10.2317 (84.8%). Per arm (the `validity arm=` lines' `usd=`, `e3-2` plus `e3-3`): `bko` $3.3736 + $3.3783 = $6.7519; `bko-population` $3.4181 + $3.4207 = $6.8388; `bko-frequency` $3.4324 + $3.4327 = $6.8651; the three sum to $20.4558, E3's `usd` to the four decimals the lines carry. file time 01:51:14 EDT), its sha256 equal · the BKO arms' rows of both slices regenerate only from these two call logs; `score --replay` needs each file with its recorded sha256 · no API call, no API spend<br>`e3-4` · 2026-10-29, the merge at 01:51:29 EDT (`scratchpad/e3-4-merge.log`) and the readings from 01:51:36 EDT (line 1 of `runs/e3-4/criteria.log`) · run commit · `merge e3` over `runs/e3-2/scores.csv` and `runs/e3-3/scores.csv`, stdout redirected to `runs/e3-4/scores.csv`; no role of `bko.app.runs.ROLES` names a merge log (ADR [`tooling/run-folders`](adr/tooling/run-folders.md)), so its stderr is kept in `scratchpad/e3-4-merge.log` (gitignored, sha256 `8e0d4adfd8c87efa46a1f7b3f0b90d236d15a170b60d815325b2e775f7bc82a9`), whose three lines read `merge file=runs/e3-2/scores.csv holdouts=0-49 rows=13050 sha256=4514cdb431d2a6dc74e88ce91ba9bbdc4759ef510f18100ebc156034585296bf`, `merge file=runs/e3-3/scores.csv holdouts=50-99 rows=13050 sha256=b9cc3d8f424c919b95ac49c1d8be47e93bd9ce7f4faae677ab63e6c7d80e50c5` and `merge files=2 holdouts=100 rows=26100 sha256=b49c218239a1c3a8bec7c1276cbd535805e5296260c96c8609449473f3fe7ef7`: each input's sha256 is its slice's `scores rows=` line's, and the output's is `runs/e3-4/scores.csv`'s · the replays' scores merge to the same bytes: `merge e3 runs/e3-2-replay/scores.csv runs/e3-3-replay/scores.csv | cmp - runs/e3-4/scores.csv` exits 0 (the operator's statement) · readings: Configuration's three, at the run commit, each exit 0 (the operator's statement): (i) wrote `runs/e3-4/criteria.csv`, its header alone (sha256 `62b5db24953338359e134a38a40a1cfbda0cadc1683c174136e43ad74074a139`), since E3 holds no criterion, and `runs/e3-4/criteria.log` (sha256 `f535446d527e27b6261139398b454167f4b59eebf82bb5f04d4ca4f8be7995d6`), whose 25 `unjudged criterion=` lines read `bko` against each comparison method and 5 `beside` series read it against `real`, the 7 replica series skipped `reason=absent` since `real` has no replica; (ii) [C13] wrote `scratchpad/e3-paired.txt` (gitignored), 79 lines, sha256 `26516c561bb65fc3aff4fc5699e84a450480f7bd5183eb111a126d80f194ad03`; (iii) [C14] wrote `scratchpad/e3-means.txt` (gitignored), 10 lines, sha256 `b0f0c3aaae3c3d5922280aa1e5a9f3d918cc3d4e9a9ad738485b7702d7650a7d` · `scratchpad/e3-4-merge.log` and `runs/e3-4/criteria.log` carry a wall-clock stamp on every line, so a re-run matches them by the content after each stamp, not by sha256 · no API call, no API spend<br>Spend estimate, stated before the run (ceilings 2 x $32.50): the basis is `e1-4`, the judged run at `SETTINGS` ([E1](#e1-uci-heart-disease-augmentation) Runs row), whose call log's `end` line reads `"usd": 10.509915100000024` over `"asked": 77487`, so a record asked costs 10.509915100000024 / 77487 = $1.356346e-4 (its 23287576 input and 16362315 output tokens are 300.535 and 211.162 per record asked, at $0.10 and $0.50 per million, `bko.generate.spend.PRICES`; output is $8.1812 of the $10.5099, 77.8%). Per holdout each BKO arm asks 756 records: a replica of the thinned training split, 788 - 47 = 741 rows, plus the removed cell's 15-row fill. 3 arms x 756 records x 100 holdouts = 226800 records asked, and 226800 x $1.356346e-4 = $30.76; each slice asks 3 x 756 x 50 = 113400, half of it, $15.38, under its $32.50 ceiling. The rehearsal `e3-1`'s call log asked 226800, this quota, in 12900 calls, 129 per holdout and 6450 per slice. The basis likely overstates on the input side: `e3-1`'s requests hold 59700831 characters of system prompt and user turn over 226800 records asked, 263.23 per record asked, against `e1-4`'s 44714267 over 77487, 577.06, since a Synthea record has 9 fields against UCI's 14 and its calls ask 17.58 records each against `e1-4`'s 14.11. Output, 77.8% of `e1-4`'s spend, need not shrink with the fields, since the thinking at effort `medium` is part of it. Top-ups and refused attempts' retries add to it (`e1-4` asked 77487 against a base quota of 77400). Worst case: an attempt asks at most `E3.budget` = 3 times its rows, so a slice's hard maximum before retries is 3 x $15.38 = $46.14, above its $32.50 ceiling, which stops the slice first, as the projection after 5 holdouts does for any slice projected to reach it.<br>Duration estimate, a bracket per slice, the two slices running concurrently. Ceiling side: `e1-4`'s call log runs from its `run` line's `started` 2026-10-28T08:08:12.860632Z to its `end` line's `ended` 13:08:18.644358Z, 18005.784 s, for 77487 records asked at 8 in flight, 0.2323717 s per record asked; 113400 x 0.2323717 s = 26351 s, about 7.3 h. That rate measures `e1-4`'s wave packing, not throughput at 8 in flight: its mean in flight is 3.59, and 41% of its span ran with one call in flight ([E1](#e1-uci-heart-disease-augmentation) Runs row). Full packing: 6450 calls x `e1-4`'s mean call latency 11.772 s (request `at` to reply `at`, 5490 calls) / 8 in flight = 9491 s, about 2.6 h; E3's calls ask more records each, and live top-ups add calls. High side: G4 is the measured precedent for two concurrent runs at 8 in flight each sharing the API: `g4-1` and `g4-2` started 2 s apart (05:07:57Z and 05:07:59Z), both call logs' `end` lines read `rate_limited` false and `retried` 0, and `g4-2`'s mean call latency was 11.491 s over 1840 calls against `e1-4`'s 11.772 s; `g4-2`'s wall time, 6784.346 s for 25829 records asked, 0.2626639 s per record asked, gives 113400 x 0.2626639 s = 29786 s, about 8.3 h. The account's rate limit is not known, so G4 supports, without bounding, two slices at 8 in flight each. Local scoring: the rehearsal `e3-1` scored all 100 holdouts on canned replies in 884 s (21:20:10 to 21:34:54 EDT, its `launch.log`), about 9 s per holdout, about 7.4 min per 50-holdout slice alone; the two slices share the CPU, so each takes longer beside the other. `e1-4`'s wall time already holds its own local work, on UCI's training rows, and E3's local arms fit about three times as many (741 against 238), which the scaling does not count (the ADR, Rationale).<br>Merge: PR #57 merged as squash commit `fc9d92e`, and `git diff --stat 8a50dc6 fc9d92e -- src` prints nothing. |

## E5. Augmentation against training-set size

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Status | `complete` |
| Question | With the test split fixed, does augmentation pay more when each holdout keeps only a quarter, half or three quarters of each cell's training rows, for recall and AUC-PR over the whole test split and the flagged subgroups? |
| Configuration | Runs: `e5-quarter`, `e5-half` and `e5-threequarters` in `bko.evaluate.named_runs.RUNS` (`E5_FRACTIONS`), each `SETTINGS_SWEEP` (E1's holdouts, split, methods and `bko`, replica arms the row-adding methods and `bko`, threshold and fill target 20, a $10.00 ceiling, no criterion) with `training_fraction` 1/4, 1/2 or 3/4, at `SETTINGS`; each holdout keeps per cell the fraction of its training rows rounded half up, never below 2, the test split whole (ADR [`research/e1-evaluation-protocol`](adr/research/e1-evaluation-protocol.md), A training fraction). The held-out ratio reads the subsample, so each interval widens as rows leave. `e1-4` is the fraction-1 point. Two things move with the fraction besides the rows: more cells fall under the threshold, so the flagged scope grows (the headline reads scope `all`), and those cells ask more gap rows, so the curve also carries dose. Over E1's 100 holdouts, from `bko.evaluate.prepare.prepared` and the gap request on the UCI snapshot: fraction 1/4 flags all 8 cells on every holdout, asks 9700 gap rows and 6300 replica rows, largest held-out ratio 1.0; 1/2 flags 6, asks 5200 and 12100, ratio 0.5556; 3/4 flags 3, asks 2600 and 18000, ratio 0.3571. Estimate, before the runs: `e1-4`'s `bko` spend per gap row ($0.2879 / 2000) and per replica row ($3.1911 / 23822) times the rows asked: $2.24 (`e5-quarter`), $2.37 (`e5-half`), $2.79 (`e5-threequarters`). ## B3. CLLM arms

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Status | `complete` |
| Question | With the model, settings, holdouts and split fixed, what does asking the same model for records shown real training records, CLLM's way, give beside `bko`'s ontology guidance, and what does CLLM's curation add over the same prompt uncurated, on validity, drops, test and training copies, distance to closest record, replica fidelity and utility and E1's measures, over UCI and over the ablated Synthea frame? |
| Configuration | Arms: `cllm` and `cllm-uncurated` (`bko.evaluate.experiment.MESSAGES_ARMS`), each call guided by its cell alone and showing up to `bko.evaluate.cllm.SHOW` training records, the cell's own first; `cllm` drops each row its classifier curator confidently puts at the other label, `cllm-uncurated` keeps every one; both ask up to `CLLM_BUDGET` records per requested row (ADR [`research/cllm-arm-design`](adr/research/cllm-arm-design.md), which holds the prompt, the shown draw, the curator, the pilot that fixed the budget and each departure from CLLM). Runs: `b3-uci` and `b3-synthea` in `bko.evaluate.named_runs.RUNS`, each with arms `METHOD_ARMS`, `bko`, `cllm` and `cllm-uncurated` (`B3_ARMS`), replica arms the row-adding methods and those three, candidate `cllm`, comparators `METHOD_ARMS` and no criterion: `b3-uci` E1's holdouts, split and fill over UCI with a $20.00 ceiling; `b3-synthea` E3's removal, copy rule and Synthea frame, plus `real`, with a $35.00 ceiling. Settings: `SETTINGS` for all three Messages arms, which share one call log and ceiling; the in-run `bko` keeps `E1.budget`. The CLLM arms send training records whole ([DEVELOPMENT.md](DEVELOPMENT.md#generation), What leaves the machine); both datasets' agreements are None. Estimate, fixed before the runs (`tests/test_evaluate_experiment.py::test_each_b3_ceiling_covers_its_estimate`): per arm, `e1-4`'s $10.51 over its three Messages arms on UCI and E3's $20.46 over its three on Synthea, times 1 for `bko`, f for `cllm` and 1 for `cllm-uncurated`, f each pilot's asks per requested row, the sum over its fills and cells of rows over keep share divided by its rows (1.1401 on UCI, 1.4432 on Synthea, from the Kept and Rows counts of ADR `research/cllm-arm-design`, The pilot); plus the shown records, `e1-4`'s 5490 calls over three arms (scaled on Synthea by $20.46 over $10.51) times (f + 1), times 20 records of an assumed 60 (UCI) or 100 (Synthea) input tokens each, at $0.10 per million input tokens: b3-uci $11.47 (1.25 times, $14.34, under its $20.00 ceiling) and b3-synthea $25.22 ($31.53 under $35.00). Run ids `b3-1` (`b3-uci`) and `b3-3` (`b3-synthea`; its first launch, `b3-2`, was stopped, Runs), each rehearsed and then launched under the paid-run protocol ([DEVELOPMENT.md](DEVELOPMENT.md#evaluation)). Readings, each `compare <run> <scores> --candidate <arm> [--beside <scores> --beside-arm <arm>]` into `runs/<id>-<word>/criteria.{csv,log}`: `-cllm` (`--candidate cllm`); `-raw` (`--candidate cllm-uncurated`); `-bko` (`--candidate bko --beside` the judged scores of its dataset, `runs/e1-4/scores.csv` or `runs/e3-4/scores.csv`, `--beside-arm bko`), which checks the in-run `bko` against them; `-inrun` (`--candidate cllm --beside` the run's own `scores.csv` `--beside-arm bko`), the ontology against in-context records; `-curation` (`--candidate cllm --beside` the run's own `scores.csv` `--beside-arm cllm-uncurated`), curation alone.<br>Measures, with no verdict, per dataset and Messages arm: validity (`passed` over `generated`, both fills) and drops by reason; for `cllm`, the rows reaching its curator and those it keeps, and its keep share per fill and flagged cell (read as [DEVELOPMENT.md](DEVELOPMENT.md#evaluation) states it); test copies (the `copies` lines) and training copies (the `training` lines); the added rows' distance to closest record; replica fidelity (quality, column shapes, column pair trends) and replica utility; recall and AUC-PR at `all` and `flagged-subgroups` with the readings' intervals; on Synthea, recall on the removed cell's test cases against in-run `real`; and per-arm spend (each `validity` line's `usd=`).<br>Stops: a ceiling or projection stop ends the run for all three Messages arms and leaves that dataset's B3 unread, with no reading of a stopped run. |

On the augmented model, the ontology against in-context records and curation alone each contain zero at every scope; the intervals that exclude zero are replica ones: `cllm`'s replica is less faithful than `bko`'s on quality, column shapes and pair trends and trains a model with lower recall, and curation raises its replica's recall and AUC-PR at `all` over `cllm-uncurated`'s. |
| Read against | [RESEARCH.md section 4](RESEARCH.md#4-success-criteria); verdict: reported, no criterion applies. The CLLM arms are outside E1's comparison set, so their measures and `beside` intervals carry no verdict. On Synthea `cllm` added fewer rows than the other arms, 1348 of the gap fill's 1500 and 73074 of the replica's 74100 (Result, Shortfalls), so its contrasts compare a smaller fill |

## G1. Record generation through the Messages API

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Status | `complete` |
| Question | Does `bko.generate` turn each asked record into a candidate or a placeholder through the Messages API at each guidance level, and what does a call spend? |
| Configuration | Code: `bko.generate.messages` holds the call settings (`SETTINGS`), the structured-output schema (`record_schema`) and the guidance ladder (`Level`); `bko.generate.prompt` the prompt; `bko.generate.decode` the reading of a reply into candidates and placeholders; `bko.generate.spend.PRICES` the per-token prices each call's spend is computed at; ADR [`research/record-generation-design`](adr/research/record-generation-design.md) the design and its reasons. `temperature`, `top_p` and `top_k` are not sent. Settings (`SETTINGS`): model `claude-haiku-5-5`, effort `medium`, `max_tokens` 16000, `per_call` 20; the level is each run's. Runs `g1-1` to `g1-13` used model `claude-opus-5-5` at their commits, as each Runs row's served model records. Data, for the command runs `g1-2` to `g1-7` (each run's stderr): the UCI Heart Disease snapshot, sha256 `b17cd273da9ce1caa4710fce80227ea454d4dbf9fcbc8e6a9121672751563adc`; the default split, seed 0, 238 training and 59 test records, fingerprint `f7a1ac74a60f2702faed4a8496aa16b99c485ddffdce02287fde49e91f8480fc`. SDK: `anthropic` as `uv.lock` pins it The app's live test (`tests/test_app_live.py`) fills E1's gap request on the default split through `bko.app`, with `bko.evaluate.guided.GuidedArm` at `SETTINGS`. |

## G3. Guidance level

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Status | `complete` |
| Question | Which guidance level of `bko.generate.messages.Level` should E1's `bko` arm generate at: does a level other than `ontology` bring its replica closer to the training rows on both column shapes and column pair trends? |
| Configuration | Runs: one named run per level, `g3-cell`, `g3-coherent`, `g3-findings`, `g3-ontology`, `g3-profile` and `g3-summary` in `bko.evaluate.experiment.RUNS`, `g3-ontology` being a replicate of E1's level at the time (`ontology`), the null control, added on 2026-10-27 while the other five ran and before any of them was read, each `E1` with arms every comparison method and `bko`, replica arms the row-adding methods (`ADDS_ROWS`) and `bko`, a spend ceiling of $10.00, no criterion, and `SETTINGS` with only `guidance` set to its level. Model, data and releases are as E1's Configuration; the settings differ from `e1-3`'s only in `guidance`, so the level is the only difference from `e1-3`'s `bko`, which ran at `ontology`. Runs records why the code that renders requests is unchanged at `ontology`: for `g3-1` to `g3-5`, `g3-replay`, a free replay of `e1-3` at `188b63a`, whose `src` differs from `a155d09`'s only in a docstring (`cmp` 0 of its LR rows); for `g3-6` at `1dd6730`, `sampler-replay` (G2's Runs), the `src` diff from its commit, and the identity of `g3-6`'s requests with `e1-3`'s. Run ids and commands, one launch per level under the paid-run protocol ([DEVELOPMENT.md](DEVELOPMENT.md#evaluation)): `g3-1` `g3-cell`, `g3-2` `g3-coherent`, `g3-3` `g3-findings`, `g3-4` `g3-profile`, `g3-5` `g3-summary`, `g3-6` `g3-ontology`, each started as `uv run --no-sync python -m bko.evaluate launch g3-<n> g3-<level>` and read with `uv run --no-sync python -m bko.evaluate compare g3-<level> runs/g3-<n>/scores.csv --beside runs/e1-3/scores.csv`. A stopped run is resumed (`launch --resume`, a new run id of this entry) or reported, never relaunched fresh, and every folder is recorded in Runs. Series: each `g3-*` scores file carries the gradient-boosting rows (`-hgb`) and `e1-3`'s does not; the `beside` reading logs 12 series (`bko.evaluate.beside.PAIRED`), all logistic regression, each at family 1, and the rule reads 2 of them per level, replica column shapes and column pair trends, 10 across the five levels. Decision: A level beats `ontology` on a measure when its paired corrected interval against `e1-3`'s `bko` (`compare --beside`, `E1.confidence`, family 1 as E1 reads met) has its lower bound above 0. Keep the levels that beat it on both replica column shapes and column pair trends; if none, E1 stays at `ontology`. Among them take the level with the largest mean difference per measure; when the measures pick different levels, take the one earlier in `bko.generate.messages.LEVELS`, the less-guided level. The record states which measure decided, the multiplicity (10 intervals), and the noise floor. A level with no complete run (a projection or ceiling stop) or no `beside` interval leaves the decision open, and the record names the missing level. Noise floor: `e1-3`'s `bko` beside its `bko-population`, from `uv run --no-sync python -m bko.evaluate compare e1 runs/e1-3/scores.csv --beside runs/e1-3/scores.csv --beside-arm bko-population` (100 holdouts paired, 0 excluded): column shapes mean 0.001276 [-0.006971, 0.009522], column pair trends mean -0.000320 [-0.013013, 0.012374]. It is a method contrast, two gap definitions within one run, not run-to-run noise at one level. The null control is `g3-ontology` beside `e1-3`'s `bko` (`compare g3-ontology runs/g3-6/scores.csv --beside runs/e1-3/scores.csv`): the same level in a second session, so its intervals show run-to-run noise; the record reports it beside the floor, and it does not change the rule, which reads the five other levels. Resolution: a level's lower bound clears 0 only once its mean difference passes about the floor interval's half-width, about 0.008 on column shapes and 0.013 on column pair trends, so "E1 stays at `ontology`" cannot be told from an effect below that size. Limits: each interval is family 1; ten are read and the selection across levels is uncorrected, so under a null with independent intervals about 1 - 0.975^10 = 22% of such studies would see some single interval clear; requiring both measures per level lowers it, and the levels share one baseline, so the true rate is unknown. The rule reads replica fidelity only; augmented recall and AUC-PR are reported beside it and not read by the rule. Controls: `cell` and `coherent` are the no-ontology controls: the Result reads `bko` at `ontology` over each (the sign-flipped `--beside` of `g3-cell` and `g3-coherent`). Exclusions: Holdouts where any arm copied a test record are excluded and counted. If fewer than two remain, the `--beside` reading is refused and writes no table; the record reports the refusal and the run's own measures, from `compare g3-<level> runs/g3-<n>/scores.csv` without `--beside`. Spend estimate: per level one arm's share of `e1-3`'s spend, $4.27 (input-adjusted: `profile` about $4.42, `summary` about $4.46, `findings`/`coherent`/`cell` $3.64-$4.11; output per record assumed level-independent, unverified); total expected $19.80-$21.21 for the five levels, plus $4.27 for `g3-6` (approved by the user in session on 2026-10-27), $24.07-$25.48 in all; worst case 6 x $10.00 ceilings = $60.00. The ceiling counts logged calls; calls the SDK retries over HTTP are outside it (E1's `retried` count), so $60.00 is the ceilings' count, not a billed cap. Duration estimate: `e1-3`'s live holdouts took about 12.7 min each with three BKO arms, so one arm's share is about 7 h per run (100 x 12.7 / 3 min); if the five concurrent runs slow each other to `e1-3`'s pace, about 21 h (100 x 12.7 min). Both are estimates, not measurements. |

The lower bound clears 0 on column shapes at `cell`, `coherent`, `profile` and `summary`, and on column pair trends at `profile` and `summary` only (`cell` -0.000197, `coherent` -0.007330, `findings` -0.011760). `profile` and `summary` beat `ontology` on both measures. `summary` has the larger mean on both: column shapes 0.033547 against `profile`'s 0.027623, column pair trends 0.031410 against 0.028069. Both measures decide `summary`, so the tie-break is not reached: E1's guidance moves to `summary`. The rule does not test `summary` against `profile`; their means differ by 0.0059 and 0.0033, below the floor's half-widths (about 0.0082 and 0.0127). The floor's intervals regenerate from the command in Configuration's Noise floor; no file stores them, since `runs/e1-3/` is never written. Multiplicity: 10 intervals read, each at family 1, the selection across levels uncorrected (Configuration, Limits). The decision is the rule's reading. This entry does not change `SETTINGS`; `SETTINGS.guidance` is `summary` (`bko.generate.messages.SETTINGS`), and E1's judged run `e1-4` runs under it.<br>Controls. `bko` at `ontology` over `cell` (the sign-flipped `beside` of `g3-1`): column shapes -0.019622 [-0.028850, -0.010395], column pair trends -0.015605 [-0.031407, 0.000197]; over `coherent` (of `g3-2`): -0.017884 [-0.026969, -0.008799] and -0.008030 [-0.023391, 0.007330]. The no-ontology controls read higher fidelity than `ontology` on column shapes, both intervals wholly above it; on column pair trends both intervals contain 0. Ontology guidance did not beat its own controls on fidelity. `findings`, the first level that sends findings, reads no different from `ontology` on either measure. Only `profile` and `summary`, which add the reference group's concept shares and numeric quartiles (ADR [`research/record-generation-design`](adr/research/record-generation-design.md)), read above `ontology`.<br>Null control. `g3-6`, `ontology` in a second session, beside `e1-3`'s `bko`: its means are -0.000219 and -0.001282, with half-widths 0.0090 on column shapes and 0.0129 on column pair trends, close to the method-contrast floor's 0.0082 and 0.0127. So the run-to-run interval at one level is about the floor's width. `summary`'s means are 3.7 and 2.4 times the null control's half-widths, `profile`'s 3.1 and 2.2. |
| Read against | [RESEARCH.md: Evaluation contract](RESEARCH.md#5-evaluation-contract) (fidelity reported with no pass threshold) and this entry's decision rule; verdict: reported, no criterion applies. The decision rule, not a criterion of [RESEARCH.md](RESEARCH.md#4-success-criteria), reads the result: `summary`, decided by both replica column shapes and column pair trends |

## G4. Effort at summary

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Status | `complete` |
| Question | Does effort `low` meet `E1.validity` and stay non-inferior to `medium`, within δ, on `bko`'s three replica fidelity measures at `summary`, with the training-split bounds? |
| Configuration | Runs: `g4-low` and `g4-medium` in `bko.evaluate.experiment.RUNS`, each `SETTINGS_SWEEP`, the experiment G3's runs share: `E1` with arms every comparison method and `bko`, replica arms the row-adding methods (`ADDS_ROWS`) and `bko`, a spend ceiling of $10.00 and no criterion. Settings: `Settings(model="claude-haiku-5-5", effort="<effort>", max_tokens=16000, per_call=20, guidance=Level.SUMMARY)`, holdouts 0-99, the training-split bounds in the prompt. Model, data and releases are as E1's Configuration. `E1.confidence` and the family size are not logged: the Result cites them from `E1` at the run commit, the commit of each `scores.log`'s `versions commit=` line.<br>Run ids and commands: `g4-1` is `low` and `g4-2` is `medium`, launched together under the paid-run protocol ([DEVELOPMENT.md](DEVELOPMENT.md#evaluation)) from one detached worktree at the run commit, each as `env PYTHONPATH=<worktree>/src <repo>/.venv/bin/python -m bko.evaluate launch g4-<n> g4-<effort> --in-flight 8 --data-dir <repo>/data` (16 calls in flight in all, the rate probe before E3). Each launch is supervised (ADR [`research/concurrent-call-waves`](adr/research/concurrent-call-waves.md); [DEVELOPMENT.md](DEVELOPMENT.md#evaluation)): every `supervise` line is in the first folder's `launch.log`, `runs/g4-<n>/launch.log`, the last reading `supervise done segments=%d last=%s outcome=%s exit=%d`, and a run's folder of record is its last segment, `g4-<n>` or `g4-<n>-resume-<k>`. A stopped run is resumed by the supervisor or reported, never relaunched fresh, since a fresh run id would get its own ceiling; every segment folder is recorded in Runs. Each run is read at the run commit, `<low>` and `<medium>` being the two folders of record: `test ! -e runs/<low>/criteria.csv && test ! -e runs/<low>/criteria.log && env PYTHONPATH=<worktree>/src <repo>/.venv/bin/python -m bko.evaluate compare g4-low runs/<low>/scores.csv --beside runs/<medium>/scores.csv > runs/<low>/criteria.csv 2> runs/<low>/criteria.log`, and `g4-medium` alone into its own folder, `test ! -e runs/<medium>/criteria.csv && test ! -e runs/<medium>/criteria.log && env PYTHONPATH=<worktree>/src <repo>/.venv/bin/python -m bko.evaluate compare g4-medium runs/<medium>/scores.csv > runs/<medium>/criteria.csv 2> runs/<medium>/criteria.log`. The decision reads `runs/<low>/criteria.log`.<br>Rule, fixed before either run: `low` is chosen only if (i) and (ii) both hold. (i) `g4-low`'s `bko` validity, read as `passed`/`generated` from the `validity arm=bko` line (not its four-decimal `rate=`), is at least `E1.validity` (19/20). (ii) For each of replica `quality`, `column-shapes` and `column-pair-trends` at scope `all`, the line `beside arm=bko against=bko run=g4-low level=summary source=replica measure=<measure> scope=all` is present, reads `undefined=0`, and its `low=` exceeds -δ (`E1.confidence`, family 1). δ is half of G3's `summary` gain over `ontology` ([G3](#g3-guidance-level)): quality 0.0162, column shapes 0.0168, column pair trends 0.0157 (G3's means 0.032479, 0.033547 and 0.031410). Its basis: it is half of the point-estimate gain of the level G3 selected, and a selected level's gain is biased upward; and that gain was measured without the training-split bounds. The two conditions form an intersection-union test, so no multiplicity correction applies. Otherwise the effort is `medium`: a missing `beside` line (a skipped series), `undefined` above 0 on any of the three measures, a refused `beside` reading (fewer than 2 paired holdouts) or a run with no complete record after the supervisor's retries each leaves `medium`. The Result reports the realized number of paired holdouts J, the `paired=` count of the `beside scores_sha256=` line. Cost does not enter the decision. `SETTINGS.effort` takes the chosen effort, so `SETTINGS` holds only measured combinations, as G3's level reached it.<br>Power at equality, approximate, from a simulation no file stores. Inputs: the 100 per-holdout differences of `bko`'s replica quality, column shapes and column pair trends at scope `all`, `runs/g3-6/scores.csv` minus `runs/e1-3/scores.csv`, two runs at one level (`ontology`), so their difference is run-to-run noise; their sample covariance is the per-holdout contrast covariance. Each draw takes J holdouts of zero-mean normal contrasts with that covariance and reads them as `compare` does: the corrected (Nadeau-Bengio) interval, mean ± t·s·sqrt(1/J + r), t the 0.975 quantile of Student's t with J - 1 degrees of freedom and r = 5/18, the held-out ratio `compare` reads from the scores; the rule passes when all three lower bounds exceed -δ. The range runs between two cases: contrasts independent across holdouts, and contrasts correlated across holdouts as the corrected interval assumes, through one effect per draw shared by its holdouts with covariance r times the contrast covariance (correlation r/(1+r)). 20000 draws per case, numpy's `default_rng(1)` seeding one stream for J of 10, 20, 30 and 100 in turn, the independent case first at each J. With 100 holdouts the rule passes about 0.66 (correlated) to 0.97 (independent) of the time when `low` equals `medium`. Column pair trends is the binding measure: alone it passes about 0.66 to 0.97, while quality is the mean of the other two in these scores, so it rarely binds. So a choice of `medium` cannot be told from a loss smaller than δ. A rule reading all three mean contrasts at least 0, G4's question read literally, passes in about 34% of draws at equality; this rule is a non-inferiority reading of that question.<br>Also reported: replica utility (the six replica utility series in `bko.evaluate.beside.PAIRED`), range drops, tokens per record asked, seconds per call and spend. Seconds per call is measured from each live call's `request` and `reply` `at` stamps in the call log (the reply's `at` minus the request's, per call number); a resumed segment re-stamps the calls it replays, which read about 0 s, so they are left out. The two runs share the API concurrently, so the two efforts' latencies are not separable. A run's spend is its last segment's `end` line `usd`, cumulative across segments with carried spend included; `live_usd` is one segment's. Runs checks that the cache token counts are zero, from each reply's `usage.cache_creation_input_tokens` and `cache_read_input_tokens`, since the `end` line holds no cache totals while `usd` prices them. Exclusions: as G3's.<br>Spend estimate: `medium` about $4.33, from `g3-5`'s 404.573 input and 211.349 output tokens per record asked (11980221 and 6258470 over 29612 asked) at `bko.generate.spend.PRICES`. Its direction under the bounds is unmeasured: the bounds add prompt text, and fewer top-ups may offset it. `low` may cost more than `medium` if it drops more candidates. Total estimate about $8.66; ceilings 2 x $10.00 on priced replies. The ceiling bounds priced replies only: attempts the SDK retried that the server bills, and calls abandoned on an idle stop, are outside it. Duration: not estimated; Runs records the measured one. |

No augmented, replica-utility or `dcr-p5` interval excludes 0: augmented recall and AUC-PR, all and flagged; replica recall and AUC-PR, all and flagged; `dcr-p5` of the added records. `bko.evaluate.beside.PAIRED` holds four replica utility series at the run commit (recall and AUC-PR, each all and flagged), and all four are reported above; the 12 `beside arm=bko` lines (lines 129 to 140 of `runs/g4-1/criteria.log`) are those, three replica fidelity series, four augmented series and `dcr-p5`.<br>Decision, by the rule stated in Configuration before either run was read. (ii) The lower bound against -δ: quality -0.017570 against -0.0162 fails; column shapes -0.013422 against -0.0168 holds; column pair trends -0.024727 against -0.0157 fails. Non-inferiority is shown on column shapes and not shown on replica quality (short of -δ by about 0.0014) or column pair trends (short by about 0.0090), so condition (ii) fails on two of its three measures and the rule chooses `medium`. The half-widths (0.0091 on quality, 0.0074 on column shapes, 0.0137 on column pair trends) are each smaller than δ, so the failures come from point estimates that lean negative: quality -0.008502 and column pair trends -0.011026, about 0.52 and 0.70 of δ. Every replica contrast's mean is negative (quality, column shapes, column pair trends, recall all and flagged, AUC-PR all and flagged) and no interval excludes 0: the sweep does not show `low` worse than `medium`, and does not show it within δ on two of the three fidelity measures. Column pair trends carries the decision; quality is the mean of the other two in these scores. The power in Configuration is at equality and does not describe this observed case. The `beside` lines are lines 129 to 140 of `runs/g4-1/criteria.log`, and the means that set δ are lines 113 to 115 of `runs/g3-5/criteria.log`. The two `compare` commands ran with `PYTHONPATH` at the run commit's worktree, the operator's statement; `criteria.log` records no commit. `medium` is `SETTINGS.effort` already (`bko.generate.messages.SETTINGS`), so this entry changes no setting; `e1-4`'s configuration keeps effort `medium` and sets `SETTINGS.guidance` to `summary`. |

## G5. BKB-drawn concepts, LLM-filled fields

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Status | `complete` |
| Question | Does prescribing each record's concepts from the BKB change `bko`'s fidelity, validity and utility? |
| Configuration | Run: `g5` in `bko.evaluate.experiment.RUNS`: `E1` with candidate `bko-skeleton`, arms every comparison method plus `bko` and `bko-skeleton`, replica arms the row-adding methods (`ADDS_ROWS`) plus `bko` and `bko-skeleton`, a spend ceiling of $25.00 and no criterion. Settings: `SETTINGS`, `Settings(model="claude-haiku-5-5", effort="medium", max_tokens=16000, per_call=20, guidance=Level.SUMMARY)`, effort `medium` being [G4](#g4-effort-at-summary)'s choice, holdouts 0-99, the training-split bounds in the prompt. Model, data and releases are as E1's Configuration. The arm is read at the configuration E1 is judged on, beside an in-run `bko` control at the same settings.<br>The arm: `bko-skeleton` is a Messages arm guided by per-subgroup gaps, as `bko` is, whose every request carries, per asked record, the concepts `bko.generate.direct.sample` drew present at the attempt's seed, label-only ones left out, one line per record after the level's components (ADR [`research/record-generation-design`](adr/research/record-generation-design.md), What every level sends). Within one call a record is matched to a line by concept set, not by position, and only a record kept in the call's own cell consumes one: a record outside that cell is dropped as `dropped-cell`, one matching no line left unconsumed as `dropped-concepts`, and each line not consumed returns to the end of its cell's queue for a top-up, never redrawn (ADR [`research/e1-evaluation-protocol`](adr/research/e1-evaluation-protocol.md)). An attempt's lines are drawn at its own seed, so where `bko-skeleton` refused an attempt its retry draws at a derived seed `g2-2`'s `bko-direct` may not have drawn at: the `arm holdout=` lines (`attempt=`, `seed=`) of both runs' `scores.log` say which holdouts' draws differ.<br>Measures, with no verdict, per Messages arm: validity (`passed` over `generated`, both fills) and drops by reason, with `dropped-concepts` and its rate over `passed` (`dropped_concepts` over `passed` on the `validity arm=bko-skeleton` line); the lines re-queued (the sum of the `guided` lines' `requeued=`); replica fidelity (quality, column shapes, column pair trends) and replica utility (recall and AUC-PR, all and flagged); test copies (the `copies` lines); and per-arm spend (the `validity` line's `usd=`).<br>Stops: a ceiling stop ends the run for both Messages arms, which share one call log and ceiling, and leaves G5 unread, with no partial reading. After holdout 0's `holdout` line, the operator reads `bko-skeleton`'s `dropped_concepts` over `passed` from its `guided` lines: records asked per row run near 1 over (1 minus that rate), so a rate near 2/3 brings an attempt near `E1`'s budget of 3 records asked per row, and the operator then stops the run (F7, `kill -TERM <child_pid>`, [DEVELOPMENT.md](DEVELOPMENT.md#evaluation)) rather than spend toward a late refusal.<br>Run id `g5-1`, launched alone under the paid-run protocol ([DEVELOPMENT.md](DEVELOPMENT.md#evaluation)) with `--in-flight 8`; its folder of record is `runs/g5-1/`. Readings, at the run commit:<br>(i) the in-run control, `test ! -e runs/g5-1/criteria.csv && test ! -e runs/g5-1/criteria.log && env PYTHONPATH=<worktree>/src <repo>/.venv/bin/python -m bko.evaluate compare g5 runs/g5-1/scores.csv --beside runs/g5-1/scores.csv --beside-arm bko > runs/g5-1/criteria.csv 2> runs/g5-1/criteria.log`;<br>(ii) the sampler alone, `mkdir -p runs/g5-1-direct && test ! -e runs/g5-1-direct/criteria.csv && test ! -e runs/g5-1-direct/criteria.log && env PYTHONPATH=<worktree>/src <repo>/.venv/bin/python -m bko.evaluate compare g5 runs/g5-1/scores.csv --beside runs/g2-2/scores.csv --beside-arm bko-direct > runs/g5-1-direct/criteria.csv 2> runs/g5-1-direct/criteria.log`;<br>(iii) the judged run's `bko`, at the same settings, `mkdir -p runs/g5-1-judged && test ! -e runs/g5-1-judged/criteria.csv && test ! -e runs/g5-1-judged/criteria.log && env PYTHONPATH=<worktree>/src <repo>/.venv/bin/python -m bko.evaluate compare g5 runs/g5-1/scores.csv --beside runs/e1-4/scores.csv --beside-arm bko > runs/g5-1-judged/criteria.csv 2> runs/g5-1-judged/criteria.log` |

(b) The skeleton arm's user turn is longer by its block of lines. Over the 1800 `bko-skeleton` calls of the free rehearsal `score g5 --rehearse` at this entry's commit, the block holds 2640.8 characters per call on average (184.24 per record asked, 14.333 records asked per call), measured from the call log's `request` lines; `e1-4`'s call log gives 23287576 input tokens over 44714267 characters of system prompt and user turn, 0.52081 tokens per character, an upper rate for the block since the input tokens also count the output format. So 2640.8 x 0.52081 = 1375 more input tokens per call, and over `e1-4`'s 1830 calls per arm (5490 / 3), 1830 x 1375 = 2.517 million tokens at $0.10 per million (`bko.generate.spend.PRICES`), $0.25. Floor: $7.006 + $0.25 = $7.26. (c) A rehearsal follows every line, so it measures no concept drop. Allowance: the skeleton arm asks twice its rows, as a concept-drop rate of 1/2 of `passed` gives: $3.503 + 2 x ($3.503 + $0.25) = $11.01. An attempt asks at most 3 times its rows (`E1.budget`), so a skeleton arm that returns spends at most 3 x $3.753 = $11.26, $14.76 with `bko`; top-up calls ask fewer records each, so their fixed prompt costs more per record than this per-record basis carries, and refused attempts' retries add to it.
