## G6. Generator model

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Status | `complete` |
| Question | With everything but the generator model fixed at the judged configuration, what do Sonnet 5.5 and three open models run on this machine give on validity, drops, tokens and compute per record asked, replica fidelity and utility and each E1 measure, beside the judged Haiku 5.5 `bko` at the same holdouts? |
| Configuration | Runs: `g6-sonnet`, `g6-gemma`, `g6-nemotron` and `g6-olmo` in `bko.evaluate.named_runs.RUNS`, each `G6_BASE` (E1's holdouts, split and fill with arms `METHOD_ARMS` and `bko`, replica arms the row-adding methods and `bko`, no criterion) at `SETTINGS` with the model alone changed (`G6_MODELS`): `claude-sonnet-5-5` through the Messages API with a $90.00 ceiling, and `gemma-4-26b-a4b-q8_0`, `nemotron-3-nano-30b-a3b-q8_0` and `olmo-3-7b-instruct-q8_0` through `llama-server` on `127.0.0.1` with thinking off and a $1.00 ceiling (ADR [`research/local-generator-backend`](adr/research/local-generator-backend.md), which records each file's digest, the server build and the probes). Sonnet 5.5's prices: ADR [`research/record-generation-design`](adr/research/record-generation-design.md). Estimate: `e1-4`'s 300.535 input and 211.162 output tokens per record asked over its 25829 records asked give Sonnet 5.5 $70.07; the local models bill nothing. Opus 5.5 is not run ([IDEAS.md](IDEAS.md#opus-55-as-a-generator)). Readings, two per run, each beside `e1-4`'s `bko`: the plain reading, `compare <run> <scores> --candidate bko --beside runs/e1-4/scores.csv --beside-arm bko` into `runs/<run-id>-judged/`, which pairs only holdouts where no arm of either run copied a test record; and the shared reading (ADR [`research/shared-test-split-reading`](adr/research/shared-test-split-reading.md)), which rescores the run with `e1-4` and `e1-4` with the run on each holdout's test split less every record either copies, into `runs/<run-id>-shared/` and `runs/<run-id>-sharedbase/`, and reads the two by `compare` into `runs/<run-id>-sharedjudged/` ([DEVELOPMENT.md](DEVELOPMENT.md#evaluation) holds the commands, and the rescore with no `--with`, the self-check, writes `runs/<run-id>-selfcheck/`). On this pair the rescore's `experiment`-line check (check 2) reads `e1-4`'s line, written at commit `48098d0`, which names no `frame`, `seeds`, `removal`, `training_fraction`, `decontaminate` or `threshold`, at each key's assumed value (ADR `research/shared-test-split-reading`), and the other line holds that value; the split check (each holdout's `split` line) and the arm-line checks pin the rest. The shared readings ran at `7cfe087`, whose check 2 compared only the keys both lines name; the checks score nothing, and each G6 pair, in both directions, and each folder alone passes every check before scoring under the assumed values. Compute per record asked: tokens and FLOPs per record asked for a local model (FLOPs = 2 x active parameters x (prompt - cached + generated tokens), the cached tokens being the prompt tokens the server's usage reports as cached; active parameters from each GGUF header: Gemma 3.823B, Nemotron 3.580B, Olmo 7.298B); tokens and USD for an API model. No reading uses wall time, which depends on the machine (user decision 2026-11-01). Only `g6-2` has a rehearsal: it bills nothing, so it checks the local runs' wiring and folder roles, not their stop rules, and `g6-3` and `g6-4` ran without one. Each local run folder's `server.log` is a copy of the operator's `llama-server` serve log for that run, not a file the harness writes. `g6-3`'s and `g6-4`'s begin with a failed first start: the server was pointed at the model file under the run's worktree (`<worktree>/data/models/...`), logged `failed to open GGUF file` with `(No such file or directory)` and `exiting due to model loading error`, and the second start loads the same file from `<repo>/data/models/`. Thinking-cost runs: `g6-gemmathink` and `g6-nemotronthink` (`G6_THINKING`), each `G6_BASE` on holdouts 0 to 4 at `SETTINGS` with the model alone changed to a `-thinking` id, `gemma-4-26b-a4b-q8_0-thinking` and `nemotron-3-nano-30b-a3b-q8_0-thinking`, at a $1.00 ceiling and an idle limit of 1700 s, a liveness guard and not a measurement; each serves its base model's weights file with `--jinja --reasoning on --reasoning-format deepseek`, so the server parses the reasoning out of the reply, and the harness reads the reply's text alone (ADR [`research/local-generator-backend`](adr/research/local-generator-backend.md), Thinking variants). Their thinking-off comparison is `g6-2`'s and `g6-3`'s calls on the same holdouts, those requested before each `scores.log`'s `holdout=4 done=5/` stamp, so no thinking-off control run is made; no `compare` reading is made of either. Their compute is the formula above, the generated tokens including the reasoning; the reasoning and answer tokens of each reply are counted apart by the running server's `/tokenize` (`scratchpad/thinking-measures.py`), and the two sum to the server's `completion_tokens` within the template's separator tokens. A 100-holdout run's tokens and FLOPs are projected two ways: projection A, the per-record-asked figures over holdouts 0 to 4 times the records asked in the thinking-off 100-holdout run (`g6-2` 25879, `g6-3` 26920); projection B, the five-holdout totals times 20, which keeps the records a thinking-on run asks again. |

Thinking off (`g6-2`, holdouts 0 to 4): 95 calls, 1297 records asked; per record asked 230.193 tokens computed and 1.7601e12 FLOPs; 2.2828e15 FLOPs in all.<br>Nemotron, thinking on (`g6-6`): 349 calls, 6024 records asked; per record asked 749.116 generated tokens (714.899 reasoning, 34.055 answer), 816.640 tokens computed and 5.8471e12 FLOPs; 3.5223e16 FLOPs in all. Thinking off (`g6-3`, holdouts 0 to 4): 117 calls, 1358 records asked; per record asked 257.081 tokens computed and 1.8407e12 FLOPs; 2.4997e15 FLOPs in all.<br>FLOPs per record asked roughly triple with thinking on for both models. Nemotron's total over holdouts 0 to 4 is about 14 times its thinking-off total (3.5223e16 against 2.4997e15), since it asked 6024 records against 1358: 197 of `g6-6`'s 349 replies stopped at `max_tokens` (16000) and decoded no record, and the fill asked those records again; its replies that ended normally decoded 99%. One of `g6-5`'s 92 replies stopped at `max_tokens`.<br>Validity of the `bko` arm (sums of each `scores.csv`; gap fill, then replica fill): Gemma thinking on 100 of 100 and 1191 of 1211 (1 range drop); Nemotron thinking on 100 of 172 and 2026 of 5863 (1 quota drop).<br>Projected 100-holdout run (Configuration; each product taken at the unrounded per-record rate). Projection A: Gemma 583.757 x 25879 = 1.5107e7 generated tokens and 5.3464e12 x 25879 = 1.3836e17 FLOPs; Nemotron 749.116 x 26920 = 2.0166e7 generated tokens and 5.8471e12 x 26920 = 1.5741e17 FLOPs. Projection B: Gemma 1311 x 20 = 26220 records asked, 765305 x 20 = 1.5306e7 generated tokens and 7.0091e15 x 20 = 1.4018e17 FLOPs; Nemotron 6024 x 20 = 120480 records asked, 4512674 x 20 = 9.0253e7 generated tokens and 3.5223e16 x 20 = 7.0446e17 FLOPs. The two projections differ because projection A asks the thinking-off run's records once each, while projection B keeps the records a thinking-on run asks again after a reply stops at `max_tokens`; they nearly agree for Gemma, whose thinking-on run asked 1311 records against `g6-2`'s 1297 on the same holdouts, and differ by about 4.5 times for Nemotron.<br>Against the thinking-off 100-holdout runs (`g6-2` 4.4676e16 FLOPs in all, 1.7263e12 per record asked; `g6-3` 4.7549e16, 1.7663e12): Gemma's projections are about 3.1 times `g6-2`'s total under A and B alike; Nemotron's are about 3.3 times `g6-3`'s under A and about 14.8 times under B. |

## G7. BKB likelihood curation

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Status | `complete`: phase 1 ran and did not separate, so phase 2 (a paid curated arm) does not run |
| Question | Does a candidate's log-probability under the fused BKB's concept draw separate the `bko` arm's records that match the real cell from those that do not, so that keeping the highest-scoring records would be worth a paid arm? |
| Configuration | Score, pool, split and gate as ADR [`research/bkb-candidate-curation`](adr/research/bkb-candidate-curation.md) fixes them before the run: each record's log-probability under `bko.gaps.likelihood.Likelihood` at its cell's shares (`bko.gaps.shares.shares`); per holdout, the `bko` arm's replica records whose outcome is `kept` or `dropped-quota`; each cell split at its own median, ties in `sha256(f"{holdout}:{call}:{position}")` order; quality, column shapes and column pair trends (`bko.evaluate.fidelity`, SDMetrics 0.32.0) of the pooled tops and pooled bottoms against the holdout's training rows, paired top minus bottom over E1's 100 holdouts with the corrected interval at `E1.confidence`, family 3. The gate separates when all three intervals lie above 0; a failure reads as "the score does not separate fidelity at the median". Command: `uv run --no-sync python -m bko.evaluate likelihood e1 --candidates runs/e1-4/holdout-candidates.jsonl` ([DEVELOPMENT.md](DEVELOPMENT.md#evaluation)); no API call |

## C1. Gap closure after fusion

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Status | `complete` |
| Question | Do at least half of the per-subgroup gaps of the UCI Heart Disease training split close once the generated records that pass validation are fused back into the BKO, and how many close, stay open or appear under each other gap definition? |
| Configuration | Commands, both from one commit, as [DEVELOPMENT.md](DEVELOPMENT.md#gap-closure) gives them: `python -m bko.generate per-subgroup --guidance summary --request completeness`, which spends, then `python -m bko.fusion` over its stdout, which does not; the acceptance's one command is the second, and the first records its input. Code and design: `bko.gaps.definitions.completeness_request`, `bko.generate.candidates`, `bko.fusion`; ADR [`research/gap-closure-design`](adr/research/gap-closure-design.md) states which records join and how closure is read, and ADR [`research/bko-formal-gap-definition`](adr/research/bko-formal-gap-definition.md) the request. Data: the UCI archive `bko.datasets.sources` names and the default split, seed 0, each identity copied from the run's stderr; releases as `bko.datasets.sources` pins them. Generator: `bko.generate.messages.SETTINGS` at each run's commit, as its Runs row's served model records, at the level its row states (`c1-0`'s candidates at `cell` and `ontology`, `c1-1` and `c1-2` at `ontology`, `c1-3` at `summary`, `SETTINGS`' level), guided by the per-subgroup gaps, whose findings block names the field states that ground each gap (ADR [`research/record-generation-design`](adr/research/record-generation-design.md)). Request: each cell holding a per-subgroup gap in the default training split, asked for `UNDERREPRESENTED_BELOW` rows rather than its shortfall; it is not E1's request, so G1's candidates do not answer it. How the readings [RESEARCH.md](RESEARCH.md#4-success-criteria) leaves open are fixed: the detected gaps are the per-subgroup (cell, statement) pairs of the default training split; a pair closes when detection over the training and joined records no longer lists it; the criterion is met when twice the closed per-subgroup pairs is at least the pairs listed before, and not met otherwise, with no interval, since one judged run is read; population-level and frequency-aware closure are reported beside it and judge nothing, population-level listing no gap on this split before the join ([E2](#e2-synthea-gap-detection-ablation)), so its share is undefined; count-based closure is not read, since this request fills cells by gap, not by count. The judged run: the first paid run that exits 0 with its call log ending `complete`; a run that fails or is interrupted after a call reached the API keeps its row with the reason, and the paid command runs once more under a new run id; a run whose calls returned is judged as it stands, placeholders included; a later paid run supersedes the judged run only when a change to `bko.generate` changes what a call sends (the request parameters the call log records, or the quota), and any other paid run adds a row and changes no verdict; a change to the reading reruns the free command over the same candidates file and adds a row. Limits: closure depends only on which mapped concepts the joined records show; per-subgroup and population-level pairs cannot become new when records join, so a new count of 0 under either checks the implementation; frequency-aware reference shares move with any same-label cell's records, so it opens and closes pairs in cells that received none; `c1-0` closed the same per-subgroup pairs with G1's `cell`-level candidates, whose prompt names no gap, as with its `ontology`-level ones, so the result says the pipeline closes the gaps it detects, not that ontology guidance closes them, nor that a closed gap was filled with clinically right records; one run, no seed; the candidates file is gitignored, so its sha256 in Runs is the record. These readings were fixed after `c1-0`; the definition, split and request were fixed by ADR `research/bko-formal-gap-definition` before it |

## D1. Graded grounding and the frequency-aware level

| Field | Entry |
|---|---|
| Status | `complete` |
| Question | How do the per-subgroup and population-level definitions read E2's ablations when a statement needs at least `k` records to be grounded, how does the frequency-aware definition read them at other posterior levels, and which level does the rule stated here choose? |
| Configuration | Commands: `uv run --no-sync python -m bko.gaps synthea` and `uv run --no-sync python -m bko.gaps uci`, the same commands and inputs as [E2](#e2-synthea-gap-detection-ablation), whose Configuration holds the population, releases, code mappings, scope, ablations, truth and cells; the sweep logs to stderr only, so each stdout matches E2's at the same inputs. Code: `bko.gaps.sweep` holds the settings and the rule; `bko.gaps.definitions.gaps` takes the grounding count as `grounding` and the level as `credibility`, defaults 1 and `CREDIBILITY`, the rules E2 ran (ADRs [`research/bko-formal-gap-definition`](adr/research/bko-formal-gap-definition.md) and [`research/frequency-aware-gap-score`](adr/research/frequency-aware-gap-score.md)). Settings: per-subgroup and population-level at each grounding count of `GROUNDING_COUNTS` (1, 2, 3 and 5) at the default level, a concept grounding statements in a fragment only when at least `k` of its records show it and a concept a cell assumes held at every `k`; frequency-aware at each level of `CREDIBILITY_LEVELS` (0.90, 0.95 and 0.99), which ignores the grounding count. Reading: on Synthea, every setting over every ablation of `E2_ABLATIONS`, the whole removals and every fraction of both, as sensitivity and specificity against the ablation's truth, as E2 defines them (`bko.gaps.experiment.detection`), from the `sweep ablation=` lines of stderr; the truth does not depend on the setting; on UCI, every setting's gap and cell counts on the default training split, seed 0, from the `sweep dataset=` lines. Rule, fixed before the run: the one the Level bullet of ADR [`research/frequency-aware-gap-score`](adr/research/frequency-aware-gap-score.md) states, `n/a` cases included; the `chosen` line of `synthea`'s stderr records its outcome with the measures that decided it ([DEVELOPMENT.md](DEVELOPMENT.md#gap-detection) reads its fields). For the grounding definitions every `k` is reported and none is chosen: E1's judged arm keeps `k` of 1. `bko-frequency` reads the chosen level only once [RESEARCH.md: Detect](RESEARCH.md#detect) says so. Each Runs row names its commit, which stderr does not log, and copies the population, release and archive identities from its stderr |
